## Semantic Chunks
### Section: Introduction
- **Level**: `intro`
- **Hash**: `47ef483ce1699022eeccf7a110326f2305f88e86942e84f1d0365ecc638b7bf2`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/how-much-matching-text-is-enough-to-investigate-plagiarism` (Similarity: 0.9222)

```text
Showing the exact original source with side-by-side text comparisons is far more useful than an abstract similarity percentage because it provides verifiable physical proof, distinguishes mechanical citation errors from intentional theft, and turns integrity reviews into constructive learning moments.

In traditional academic integrity workflows, teachers were handed a single similarity number—such as "22% Match" —which often triggered confusion rather than clarity. A 22% score could mean a student included four properly formatted direct quotes, or it could mean they copied their entire thesis statement from an uncited journal. Conversely, an essay with only a 5% score could contain a single plagiarized paragraph that represented the entire core argument. Evidence-Based Reporting eliminates this ambiguity by focusing on verifiable source text and drafting context.

Below is an in-depth analysis of why showing original sources and writing playback provides superior clarity over legacy percentage metrics.

Checkmark Plagiarism powers evidence-based reporting by pairing plagiarism detection with essay writing playback , AI detection , autograding , and integrations with Canvas and Google Classroom .
```

### Section: The 4 Reasons Exact Sources Outperform Similarity Scores
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `f39206df722167b956c380dbe477f24587726611a9b6dc0b0199654287e4963d`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/can-a-plagiarism-checker-show-me-the-exact-source` (Similarity: 0.8814)

```text
1. Eliminates Score Ambiguity

Percentages combine bibliography entries, prompt text, properly cited quotes, and actual plagiarism into one number. Showing the source isolates the exact stolen text immediately.

2. Prevents Parent & Student Disputes

You cannot argue with a side-by-side text comparison displaying matching paragraphs and a live URL. Evidence cards turn subjective debates into objective reviews.

3. Distinguishes Intent from Error

Allows teachers to see if matching text was an uncredited copy-paste or a legitimate paraphrased source missing a parenthetical citation.

4. Transforms Integrity into Pedagogy

Enables educators to show students exactly how their paraphrasing was too close to the original source, teaching proper synthesis and citation skills.
```

### Section: The Power of Side-by-Side Text Alignment
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `b89d2ca6de584558aeff5ff7df38680b965f6c6265c55ad0be1ee66749e5c99b`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/how-side-by-side-source-quotations-protect-students-from-unfair-similarity-accusations` (Similarity: 0.8965)

```text
When an instructor opens a Checkmark Source Evidence Card , the interface highlights matching words, synonyms, and omitted phrases in color:

Direct Quotation Check: If the student placed quotation marks around the text and included a citation, the teacher can dismiss the card with one click. 
 Patchwriting Identification: If the student merely swapped every third word with a thesaurus synonym, the underlying sentence structure is clearly visible. 
 Writing Playback Corroboration: Teachers can scrub the timeline to confirm whether the matched text was typed keystroke-by-keystroke or inserted via external clipboard paste.

Read more in how Checkmark writing process analysis works .
```

Similarity Percentage Only (Legacy Approach)

Outputs an abstract number (e.g., "27% Similarity"). 
 Treats cited quotations and stolen text identically. 
 Vulnerable to "safe score" misunderstandings (e.g., 
 Leaves teachers guessing about the true extent of misconduct.

Checkmark Evidence-Based Reporting (Modern)

Interactive side-by-side highlighted text comparison cards. 
 Clickable destination URLs and original publication dates. 
 Correlated with keystroke writing playback and paste logs. 
 Provides indisputable proof for conferences and conduct boards.

### Section: A 5-Step Educator Protocol for Evidence-Based Reviews
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `5da1b5b0917eb1c1ba8bad156a23bd795e2e9066af51e4e66e5571bc51a71619`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/what-order-should-teachers-review-evidence-of-ai-use` (Similarity: 0.9016)

```text
Educator Evidence-Based Review Protocol:

1. Open the Checkmark Report in Canvas SpeedGrader or Google Classroom. 
 2. Ignore the raw percentage score and inspect the individual Source Evidence Cards. 
 3. Dismiss properly cited direct quotations and bibliography items with one click. 
 4. Examine uncredited matches side-by-side with original web text. 
 5. Review the Essay Playback timeline to verify the keystroke origin of the matched text.
```
